<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that the standard Salvetti complex of every Artin group with finitely many standard generators is aspherical. This resolves the Artin $`K(\pi,1)`$ conjecture in finite rank.

- **claim-main-aspherical** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every Coxeter matrix on a finite set $S$, the universal cover of $X(W,S)$ is contractible. Equivalently, $$\pi_n\bigl(X(W,S)\bigr)=0\qquad(n\ge2).$$ The conclusion allows arbitrary finite labels, infinite labels, and connected or disconnected diagrams.
  - Explanation: For any Coxeter matrix on a finite generating set, the universal cover of the Salvetti complex is contractible, so higher homotopy groups vanish.
- **claim-parabolic-embedding** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-topology-parabolic-embedding)): For every $T\subseteq S$, the homomorphism from the Artin group defined by the submatrix on $T$ to $A$, sending its standard generators to the corresponding $\sigma_t$, is injective. Its image is $A_T$.
  - Explanation: Standard parabolic Artin subgroups embed injectively, with no sphericity assumption.
- **claim-finite-monoid** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-topology-finite-monoid)): Suppose that $T$ is spherical, and let $A_T^+$ be its positive Artin monoid. Then the following statements hold.* 1. *The natural map $A_T^+\to A_T$ is injective. In particular, $A_T^+$ is cancellative on both sides.* 2. *Every element of $A_T$ can be written both as $ab^{-1}$ and as $c^{-1}d$, with $a,b,c,d\in A_T^+$.* 3. *Any two elements of $A_T^+$ have a greatest common right divisor, and also a greatest common left divisor.* 4. *If $w_T$ is the longest element of $W_T$, then $\Delta_T=\iota(w_T)$ is the finite-type fundamental element. Its left divisors and its right divisors coincide and are exactly the elements $\iota(w)$, $w\in W_T$.
  - Explanation: For spherical subsets the positive monoid injects, is cancellative, has fractions, common divisors and a fundamental element.
- **claim-spherical-asphericity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-topology-spherical-asphericity)): If $T$ is spherical, the universal cover of the CW complex $X(W_T,T)$ with the preceding permutahedral attaching maps is contractible.
  - Explanation: Classical spherical theorem: the finite-type Salvetti complex has contractible universal cover.
- **claim-salvetti-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-salvetti-comparison)): There is a homotopy equivalence $$\widetilde X(W,S)\simeq\|\mathcal D\|.$$ Consequently, contractibility of $\|\mathcal D\|$ implies the conclusion of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.
  - Explanation: The universal cover is homotopy equivalent to the order complex of the spherical-coset poset.
- **claim-cartan-positive-null** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-cartan-positive-null)): For a spherical subset $T\subseteq S$, the inverse $C_T^{-1}$ is entrywise nonnegative, and is strictly positive within each connected diagonal block. Prescribing the coordinates of $p$ outside $T$ therefore determines a unique vector harmonic on $T$, by which we mean $(Cp)\|_T=0$. Nonnegative prescribed coordinates give a nonnegative harmonic extension.* *More generally, let $G$ be a nonempty connected vertex set. If $v_i>0$ for $i\in G$ and $C_Gv\geq0$ coordinatewise, then either $C_G$ is positive definite or $C_Gv=0$.
  - Explanation: On spherical sets the inverse Cartan matrix is nonnegative, giving unique nonnegative harmonic extension.
- **claim-fusion-category** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-fusion-construction)): There is a finite semisimple spherical $\mathbb C$-linear tensor category $\mathscr C$ with strictly positive real dimensions, and, for every ordered edge $(s,t)$ of $\widehat S$, an object $D^1_{st}$ of dimension $b_{st}$, with $D^1_{ts}$ its dual, having the following properties. For every finite edge and simple objects $L,L'$, $$\dim_\mathbb C\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\mathscr C}(L,D^1_{st}\otimes L')\leq1.$$ On a label-$3$ edge or a frame edge one may take $D^1_{st}=\mathbf1$; on an infinite edge one may take $D^1_{st}=\mathbf1\oplus\mathbf1$.
  - Explanation: A finite semisimple spherical tensor category with positive dimensions and edge objects of prescribed dimension exists.
- **claim-frobenius-trace** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-category-frobenius)): The functional $\tau:Z\to\mathbb C$ defined by $$\tau(t_{s,L})=w_L, \qquad \tau(Z^0\oplus Z^1)=0$$ is a symmetric Frobenius trace of degree $-2$. Thus $\theta_\alpha=t_\alpha/w_L$ has trace one. The radical of $Z$ is $Z^{>0}$, and the socle of each indecomposable left or right projective is its token subspace.* *For every finite graded bimodule $M$, the adjoint dual has a natural bimodule identification $$M^\vee:=\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_{Z\text{-left}}(M,Z) \cong D_\mathbb C(M)\langle2\rangle, \label{eq:category-adjoint-duality}$$ where $D_\mathbb C$ is graded vector-space duality and $\langle a\rangle$ raises degrees by $a$. It is an involutive contravariant equivalence, fixes the regular bimodule, and satisfies $$(P_\alpha\otimes_\mathbb CQ_\beta)^\vee \cong(P_\beta\otimes_\mathbb CQ_\alpha)\langle-2\rangle. \label{eq:category-projective-dual}$$ In particular this duality preserves additive radicals.
  - Explanation: The graded algebra has a symmetric Frobenius trace; socles are token subspaces and duality is a shifted vector duality.
- **claim-finite-unfolding** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-finite-unfolding)): If $J\subseteq S$ is spherical, then $\widetilde C_{\widetilde J}$ is positive definite and simply laced, and defines a finite Coxeter system. Replacing each original letter $s_i$, $i\in J$, by the product of the commuting reflections in all vertices of its color gives a lift of words to this system. For every such word representing $w$, its lifted product $\widetilde w$ has $$D_L(\widetilde w)=\widetilde{D_L(w)},\qquad D_R(\widetilde w)=\widetilde{D_R(w)}. \label{eq:category-descents}$$ Reduced expressions lift to reduced expressions, and a reduced expression for the longest element of $W_J$ lifts to a reduced expression for the longest element of the unfolded system.
  - Explanation: For spherical J the unfolded Cartan matrix is positive definite and simply laced; lifts preserve descents and reducedness.
- **claim-twist-inverse** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-category-twist-inverse)): There are bigraded bimodule chain homotopy equivalences $$B_\alpha\otimes_ZD_\alpha\simeq Z \simeq D_\alpha\otimes_ZB_\alpha.$$
  - Explanation: The evaluation and coevaluation twist bimodules are mutually inverse up to homotopy.
- **claim-local-braid** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-category-local-braid)): If there is no arrow between distinct unfolded vertices $\alpha,\beta$, then $B_\alpha B_\beta\simeq B_\beta B_\alpha$. If there is exactly one arrow in each direction, then $$B_\alpha B_\beta B_\alpha \simeq B_\beta B_\alpha B_\beta.$$ Products here and below denote tensor products over $Z$.
  - Explanation: Twists commute with no arrow and satisfy the braid relation with one arrow each way.
- **claim-twist-action** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-twist-action)): For each original color $i$, let $$B_i=\prod_{\alpha\in\widetilde{\{i\}}}B_\alpha, \qquad D_i=\prod_{\alpha\in\widetilde{\{i\}}}D_\alpha.$$ These products are independent of their order up to bimodule homotopy. The assignments $\sigma_i\mapsto B_i$ and $\sigma_i^{-1}\mapsto D_i$ define an action of the original Artin group by invertible bimodule homotopy classes. For $x\in A$ write $B_x$ for a representing complex, and write $F_i=B_i\otimes_Z-$ on left complexes.* *For every $H\in\mathscr C$, right tensoring of labels, $$T_H(P_{s,X})=P_{s,X\otimes H},$$ is an additive functor on projectives that preserves path lengths and extends to their bounded homotopy category. It commutes naturally up to homotopy with the positive and inverse color twists, and hence with each $B_x\otimes_Z-$. All the action identities above are bimodule chain homotopy equivalences, so they remain valid upon tensoring with finite complexes of modules that are not projective.
  - Explanation: Color twists give an action of the Artin group by invertible bimodule homotopy classes, commuting with label tensoring.
- **claim-layer-t-structure** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-layer-structure)): The full subcategories defined by minimal generator levels $d\leq0$ and $d\geq0$ form a bounded $t$-structure. Its truncation $\tau_{\leq k}E$ is represented by the subcomplex of a minimal model on levels at most $k$; the complementary quotient represents $\tau_{\geq k+1}E$.* *The heart is equivalent to the abelian category of finite graded coefficient arrays just described. In particular, subobjects of a pure layer, after shifting that layer into the heart, are precisely graded subrepresentations of its array. The unshifted cohomology layer at $d$ is $Y^d(E)$.
  - Explanation: Generator levels define a bounded t-structure with heart the finite coefficient arrays.
- **claim-adjacent-layers** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-layers-amplitude-extension)): Suppose that an exact functor $F$ has layer amplitude $[0,1]$. For any $E$ and any $d$, there is a short exact sequence $$\begin{split} 0\longrightarrow Y^d\bigl(F(Y^{d-1}(E))\bigr) \longrightarrow Y^d(FE) \longrightarrow Y^d\bigl(F(Y^d(E))\bigr) \longrightarrow0, \end{split} \label{eq:layers-adjacent-positive}$$ where all terms are shifted by $[d]$ when regarded as objects of the heart. In particular their generator dimensions are additive.* *For amplitude $[-1,0]$, the corresponding sequence is $$0\longrightarrow Y^d\bigl(F(Y^d(E))\bigr) \longrightarrow Y^d(FE) \longrightarrow Y^d\bigl(F(Y^{d+1}(E))\bigr) \longrightarrow0. \label{eq:layers-adjacent-negative}$$
  - Explanation: For functors of the stated amplitude, adjacent layers fit into short exact sequences.
- **claim-bottom-composition** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-layers-bottom-composition)): Suppose that each of $F_1,\ldots,F_r$ is exact and has no negative change of layer level. Starting from a pure layer at $d$, the level-$d$ part of $F_r\cdots F_1$ is obtained by retaining only the level-$d$ part after each intermediate operation. In particular, any relation between such composed functors gives the corresponding relation between their operations on the bottom layer.
  - Explanation: The bottom layer of a composite is computed by keeping only the bottom layer at each step.
- **claim-extremal-tests** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-layers-extremal-tests)): For a pure layer $M$ at level $d$, $$H^d(e_\alpha M)\cong\operatorname{soc}_\alpha M, \qquad H^{d+2}(e_\alpha M)\cong (\operatorname{top}_\alpha M)\langle2\rangle, \label{eq:layers-extremal-tests}$$ as graded multiplicity spaces, with their corresponding placements. The shift on the top is the internal placement of the token. If a minimal complex $E$ has no generators above $d$ at $\alpha$ or its neighbors, its test in degree $d+2$ is still $(\operatorname{top}_\alpha Y^d(E))\langle2\rangle$, and all higher test cohomology there vanishes. If it has no generators below $d$ at those vertices, its test in degree $d$ is the socle of $Y^d(E)$ at $\alpha$.* *A nonzero pure layer supported on a vertex set has a nonzero top and a nonzero socle somewhere in that set.
  - Explanation: Extreme-degree cohomology of test complexes computes socle and top of a pure layer.
- **claim-positive-layer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-positive-layer)): Each $F_i$ has layer amplitude $[0,1]$. Its upper piece on a pure layer $M$ at $d$ is supported on the fiber of $i$, has no arrows, and has multiplicity $\dim\operatorname{top}_\alpha M$ at a vertex $\alpha$ of that fiber. Its bottom piece has unchanged multiplicities at all other vertices, and at $\alpha$ in the fiber it has multiplicity $$\dim Y^d(F_iM)_\alpha =\sum_\beta(2\mathop{\mathrm{id}}-\widetilde C)_{\alpha\beta} \dim M_\beta -\dim M_\alpha+\dim\operatorname{top}_\alpha M. \label{eq:layers-positive-dimension}$$ The bottom piece has zero socle in color $i$. If $M$ has zero socle in every color of a set $J$ containing $i$, its bottom piece after $F_i$ also has zero socle in all of $J$.* *For any bounded left complex $E$, without a projectivity assumption on its terms, there is a natural homotopy equivalence of test complexes $$e_\alpha F_iE\simeq(e_\alpha E)\langle2\rangle[1] \qquad(\alpha\text{ of color }i). \label{eq:layers-positive-test-shift}$$ There is the identical statement for right complexes tested on the right.
  - Explanation: A positive color twist has layer amplitude zero to one, acts by reflection on counts and preserves socle vanishing.
- **claim-inverse-amplitude** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-layers-inverse-amplitude)): If an exact equivalence $F$ has layer amplitude $[0,1]$, its inverse has amplitude $[-1,0]$. In particular each $F_i^{-1}$ has that amplitude and satisfies $$e_\alpha F_i^{-1}E\simeq (e_\alpha E)\langle-2\rangle[-1] \qquad(\alpha\text{ of color }i), \label{eq:layers-negative-test-shift}$$ with the analogous formula for right complexes.* *For either sign, acting in color $i$ leaves the generator multiplicities in every layer unchanged at all other colors.
  - Explanation: The inverse of an exact equivalence of positive amplitude has the opposite amplitude.
- **claim-height-change** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-layers-height-change)): For a positive unfolded letter $\alpha$, if $h_J(X)$ is finite, $$h_J(X)\leq h_J(B_\alpha X)\leq h_J(X)+1.$$ An increase occurs exactly when $\alpha$ is an old highest-test vertex; in that case it is the only new highest-test vertex. Vanishing of all tests persists. The same assertions hold for right actions.
  - Explanation: A positive unfolded letter raises test height by at most one, exactly when it was a highest-test vertex.
- **claim-top-divisor** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-top-divisor)): Let $b$ be a positive word in the unfolded finite-type Artin generators. If $$h_J(B_bX)>h_J(X),$$ then every unfolded vertex attaining the highest test cohomology of $B_bX$ is a left divisor of $b$ in the positive Artin monoid. For a right action, every such vertex is a right divisor of $b$. These assertions hold for arbitrary inputs with bounded test cohomology, including complexes of right simples.
  - Explanation: When a positive word raises test height, every top vertex divides the word on the matching side.
- **claim-reduced-amplitude** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-reduced-amplitude)): For spherical $J$, a positive lift of any reduced expression in $W_J$ has layer amplitude $[0,1]$. A negative lift of a reduced expression has amplitude $[-1,0]$.
  - Explanation: Positive lifts of reduced expressions have positive amplitude; negative lifts the opposite.
- **claim-tensor-truncation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-height-data-tensor-truncation)): For $H\in\mathscr C$, right tensoring of all projective labels by $H$ defines a $t$-exact functor $T_H$ for the layer $t$-structure. It commutes naturally with its truncations, layers, and all the color twist functors and their inverses.* *In particular, if a subobject is constructed by applying a word of twists, taking a layer truncation, and applying the inverse word, this construction commutes with $T_H$ as a morphism into the ambient object. No choice of minimal model or Gaussian elimination is required to be natural.
  - Explanation: Right label tensoring is t-exact and commutes with truncations, layers and twists.
- **claim-family-proportionality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-family-proportionality)): There are nonnegative vectors $m^d(x)\in\mathbb R^{\widehat S}$ such that the weighted generator dimension vector of the layer family is $$\sum_L w_L\dim Y_L^d(x)=\phi m^d(x). \label{eq:height-data-proportionality}$$ The weighted top and socle dimension vectors of these layers also have the form $\phi v$. Consequently, if a top or socle occurs anywhere in a color in the family, then at every unfolded vertex of that color it occurs in at least one member of the family.* *For each $x$ the vectors $m^d(x)$ have finite support in $d$. Uniformly over $x$, $d$, and the colors, each coordinate has only finitely many possible values in a bounded interval. Operations on the left by $\sigma_i^{\pm1}$ change $m^d$ only in coordinate $i$, and $$m_o^d(x)= \begin{cases}1,&d=0,\\0,&d\ne0.\end{cases} \label{eq:height-data-frame-coordinate}$$ On folded signed counts, either twist in color $i$ acts by the coordinate reflection for $C$: $$\sum_d(-1)^d m^d(\sigma_i^{\pm1}x) =s_i\left(\sum_d(-1)^d m^d(x)\right). \label{eq:height-data-signed-reflection}$$
  - Explanation: Weighted generator counts of the frame family are proportional to nonnegative folded vectors with finite support, frame coordinate in layer zero, and reflection action.
- **claim-capped-layer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-height-data-capped-layer)): Let $E$ be a bounded minimal projective complex with no generators above $q$ on $J\cup\partial J$. If $G$ is a word of negative twists in $J$, then $$Y^q(GE)\simeq Y^q\bigl(G(Y^q(E))\bigr). \label{eq:height-data-capped-layer}$$ Moreover $GE$ still has no generators above $q$ on $J\cup\partial J$.
  - Explanation: Negative twists in J preserve the cap and commute with taking the top allowed layer.
- **claim-cap-accessibility** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-cap-accessibility)): Let $J\subseteq S$ be spherical, let $q\in\mathbb Z$, and suppose the data at $x$ are capped at $q$ on $\partial J$. There exists $z\in A_Jx$ whose data are capped at $q$ on $J\cup\partial J$.* *Every negative generator in $J$ preserves an existing cap at $q$ on $J\cup\partial J$. At a representative with this joint cap, a positive $F_i$, $i\in J$, also preserves it whenever $$\operatorname{top}_\alpha Y_L^q=0 \qquad\text{for every $L$ and every $\alpha$ of color $i$}.$$
  - Explanation: A cap on the boundary can be upgraded to a joint cap on the type and boundary inside the coset.
- **claim-convex-subobjects** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-convex-subobjects)): Let $J\subseteq S$ be spherical, and suppose that at $x$ the data are capped at $q$ on $J\cup\partial J$. Let $p$ agree with $m^q(x)$ outside $J$ and be harmonic on $J$, and put $e=m^q(x)-p$, which is supported on $J$. Assume that for *every* representative $z\in A_Jx$ whose data are capped at $q$ on $J\cup\partial J$, $$m_j^q(z)\geq p_j\qquad(j\in J). \label{eq:height-data-universal-cap-inequality}$$ Then $e_J$ belongs to the convex hull in $\mathbb R^J$ of vectors $s_J$ with the following property: $s$ is zero outside $J$, and there are graded subrepresentations $$\mathcal S_L\subseteq Y_L^q(x),\qquad \mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\mathcal S_L\subseteq\widetilde J, \qquad \sum_Lw_L\dim\mathcal S_L=\phi s. \label{eq:height-data-convex-subrepresentations}$$ One may use a finite collection of such vectors, together with the zero vector.* *If all inequalities in <a href="#eq:height-data-universal-cap-inequality" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:height-data-universal-cap-inequality">[eq:height-data-universal-cap-inequality]</a> are strict, $e_J$ is in the ordinary interior in $\mathbb R^J$ of such a convex hull. For $J=\varnothing$ the interior convention is that $\{0\}$ is open in $\mathbb R^0$.
  - Explanation: The excess over the harmonic vector lies in the convex hull of subrepresentation dimension vectors, in the interior when bounds are strict.
- **claim-candidate-uniqueness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-candidate-uniqueness)): There is at most one candidate in $A_Tx_0$.
  - Explanation: A spherical coset contains at most one candidate.
- **claim-right-duality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-right-duality)): For every $h\in A_T$, every $\ell,\beta\in\widetilde T$, and every integer $r$, $$\label{eq:two-point-duality} K^r_{\ell\beta}(h) =K^{-2-r}_{\beta\ell}(h^{-1}).$$ If $J,J'\subseteq T$, the rows with indices outside $\widetilde J'$ are unchanged by left multiplication of $h$ by $A_{J'}$, and the columns with indices outside $\widetilde J$ are unchanged by right multiplication of $h$ by $A_J$.
  - Explanation: Right projective generator counts of an element and its inverse are related by a reversal of total degree.
- **claim-negative-bound** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-two-point-negative-bound)): Let $R$ be a minimal complex with a single simple $U_\ell$ at $(0,0)$ and shifted right projectives, and let $r\ge-1$ bound its normalized levels. Applying a right inverse twist, and then minimizing, does not increase this bound. The same holds for the product of inverse twists in one color. At the acted vertex $\alpha$, the test complexes satisfy $$\label{eq:two-point-inverse-test} (R D_\alpha)e_\alpha \simeq (Re_\alpha)\langle-2\rangle[-1].$$
  - Explanation: A right inverse twist plus minimization does not raise the normalized level bound.
- **claim-tensor-filtration** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-two-point-tensor-filtration)): With these definitions, $$\label{eq:two-point-highest-tensor} H_2(N,M)=0, \qquad H_1(N,M)\text{ survives in actual total degree }r+q+1.$$ Moreover, at every unfolded projective vertex $\beta$, the length-two cokernel surjects onto the tensor product of the right and left top quotients at $\beta$. Consequently, if a right top at level $r$ occurs in a color $j\in J$, then all the left-family tops at level $q$ in that color vanish.
  - Explanation: At the highest pair of levels length-two cohomology vanishes, so right and left tops exclude each other in a color.
- **claim-remove-tops** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-two-point-remove-tops)): By replacing $z$ within $A_Jz$, preserving the cap on $T$, one can arrange that the highest normalized right layer has no right top at any vertex of $\widetilde J$.
  - Explanation: Positive moves inside the coset remove highest right tops on the type while keeping the cap.
- **claim-subrep-tensor** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-two-point-subrepresentation-tensor)): For every family $\mathcal S\subseteq M$ in <a href="#eq:two-point-subfamilies" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:two-point-subfamilies">[eq:two-point-subfamilies]</a>, $$\label{eq:two-point-subrepresentation-homology} H_2(N,\mathcal S)=0, \qquad H_0(N,\mathcal S)\hookrightarrow H_0(N,M).$$
  - Explanation: For subrepresentation families length-two cohomology vanishes and degree-zero cohomology injects into the whole.
- **claim-euler-estimate** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-two-point-euler-estimate)): At the representative with no highest right tops on $J$, $$\label{eq:two-point-estimate} \frac1{D_0}\dim_{\mathrm{wt}}H_1(N,M) \ge n_T^{\mathsf t}a +\mathbf1_{\{r=-1\}}(a')^{\mathsf t}p_T.$$
  - Explanation: An Euler characteristic argument bounds the weighted middle cohomology from below.
- **claim-greedy-factorization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-two-point-greedy)): For nonidentity simple elements $d_1,\ldots,d_k$, the factorization $d_1\cdots d_k$ is obtained by repeatedly extracting the greatest simple suffix if and only if $$\label{eq:two-point-greedy-condition} D_R(d_i)\subseteq D_L(d_{i+1})\qquad(1\le i<k).$$ Here the descent sets are those of the corresponding elements of the Coxeter group. Consequently, if an unfolded vertex right-divides the unfolding of a positive element of $A_T^+$, its original color right-divides that positive element.
  - Explanation: Greedy suffix extraction is characterized by descent-set inclusion, and unfolded right divisors reflect to original colors.
- **claim-parabolic-detection** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-parabolic-detection)): Let $h\in A_T$ and $\varnothing\ne V\subseteq T$. If $$\label{eq:two-point-zero-block} K^r_{\ell\beta}(h)=0 \qquad (r\in\mathbb Z,\ \ell,\beta\in\widetilde V),$$ then $h\in A_{T\setminus V}$.
  - Explanation: Vanishing of a block of right projective counts forces the element into the parabolic subgroup avoiding those colors.
- **claim-isolated-layer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-isolated-layer)): Let $I\subseteq S$ be nonempty and connected. Let $c_I$ be a strictly positive vector with $C_Ic_I=0$, and extend $c$ by zero on $\widehat S\setminus I$. Let $J\subseteq I$ be spherical, possibly empty, let $q>0$ be an integer, and fix a coset $A_Jx$. Suppose that the fixed data on $(I\setminus J)\cup\partial I$ are capped at $q$ and satisfy $$m^q\|_{(I\setminus J)\cup\partial I} =c\|_{(I\setminus J)\cup\partial I}. \label{eq:rigidity-exterior-data}$$ It is impossible that every representative $z\in A_Jx$ capped at $q$ on $I$ satisfies $$m_j^q(z)>c_j\qquad\text{for every }j\in J. \label{eq:rigidity-strict-hypothesis}$$ Equivalently, if $J\ne\varnothing$ and <a href="#eq:rigidity-exterior-data" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:rigidity-exterior-data">[eq:rigidity-exterior-data]</a> holds, there is a representative capped on $I$ and a color $j\in J$ with $m_j^q\le c_j$. If $J=\varnothing$, the exterior situation <a href="#eq:rigidity-exterior-data" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:rigidity-exterior-data">[eq:rigidity-exterior-data]</a> itself is impossible.
  - Explanation: Boundary data matching a positive null vector cannot be strictly exceeded on all of J by every capped representative.
- **claim-hom-pairing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-rigidity-hom-filtration)): For pure projective layers $P,Y$, including those formed from graded subrepresentations, there are perfect pairings $$\mathsf H_i^{n,v}(P,Y)\ \times\ \mathsf H_{2-i}^{-n,\,2-v}(Y,P)\longrightarrow\mathbb C.$$ Consequently, $$\begin{split} h_2(P,Y)&=h_0(Y,P),\\ (\dim P)^{\mathsf t}\widetilde C\,\dim Y &=h_0(P,Y)+h_0(Y,P)-h_1(P,Y). \end{split} \label{eq:rigidity-euler}$$ Here $\dim P$ and $\dim Y$ count projective generators, with all placements included. For any two labels $L,H$ and any levels $l,k$, $$h_1(Y_L^l,Y_H^k)=0. \label{eq:rigidity-middle-vanishing}$$ The same vanishing holds after replacing either layer by a direct summand as a pure layer.
  - Explanation: Linear Hom cohomology of pure layers has perfect pairings, an Euler identity, and vanishing middle column.
- **claim-secondary-injection** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-rigidity-secondary-injection)): Let $I\subseteq S$, and suppose the family $E_L$ is capped at $q$ on $I$. For every $L$, let $M_L$ be a direct summand of the pure layer $Y_L^q$ supported on $\widetilde I$. If $d<q$, then for every pair of labels $L,H$ there are injections $$\mathsf H_0^{n,v}(M_L,Y_H^d) \lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow \mathsf H_2^{n+1,v}(M_L,Y_H^{d-1}), \qquad n+v=d-q. \label{eq:rigidity-secondary-injection}$$ In particular, for $\mathcal M=(M_L)_L$ and $\mathcal Y^d=(Y_H^d)_H$, $$h_0^{\mathrm{wt}}(\mathcal M,\mathcal Y^d) \le h_0^{\mathrm{wt}}(\mathcal Y^{d-1},\mathcal M). \label{eq:rigidity-secondary-bound}$$
  - Explanation: Under a cap, the secondary differential injects degree-zero Hom classes into the lower target block.
- **claim-boundary-separation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-rigidity-separation)): Let $I\subseteq S$ be nonempty and connected, and let $c$ be zero off $I$ and strictly positive on $I$, with $C_Ic_I=0$. Let $J\subseteq I$. Suppose $\mathcal M=(M_L)_L$ is a family of direct summands of one pure layer of the family $E_L$, supported on $\widetilde I$, with weighted generator array $\phi m$. Assume $m-c$ is supported on $J$ and that $(m-c)_J$ is an interior point in $\mathbb R^J$ of the convex hull of finitely many vectors $s_J$ with the following properties: $s$ is zero off $J$, and $\phi s$ is the weighted generator array of a family of graded subrepresentations $\mathcal S=(S_L)_L\subseteq\mathcal M$ supported on $\widetilde J$.* *Let $\mathcal Y=\mathcal Y^d$ be any layer of the family $E_L$, and write $\phi y$ for its weighted generator array. If its socle vanishes on $J$, then $$y\|_{\partial I}=0,\qquad (Cy)_J=0,\qquad h_0^{\mathrm{wt}}(\mathcal M,\mathcal Y) =h_0^{\mathrm{wt}}(\mathcal Y,\mathcal M)=0. \label{eq:rigidity-separation-conclusions}$$ Here $\partial I$ is the outside-neighbor set, including the frame. The assertion includes the usual interior convention in $\mathbb R^0$ when $J=\varnothing$.
  - Explanation: With interior convex-hull data and socle vanishing, the layer avoids the boundary, is harmonic on J and has vanishing weighted Hom.
- **claim-vertex-filtration** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-filtration-principle)): Let $\mathcal P$ be a set-sized poset, and well-order its elements by $\prec$. For $v\in\mathcal P$, define the earlier lower and upper posets $$\mathcal L_v=\{w\in\mathcal P:w<v,\ w\prec v\},\qquad \mathcal U_v=\{w\in\mathcal P:v<w,\ w\prec v\}.$$ The link along which $v$ is added to the induced order complex on its predecessors is the simplicial join of the order complexes of $\mathcal L_v$ and $\mathcal U_v$. If one poset is empty, this link is the order complex of the other; if both are empty, the link is empty.* *Suppose each attaching link is empty or contractible. Then every component of $\|\mathcal P\|$ is contractible and contains exactly one *source*, meaning a vertex with empty attaching link. In particular, the conclusion holds if each of $\mathcal L_v$ and $\mathcal U_v$ is empty or contractible for every $v$.
  - Explanation: If every attaching link is empty or contractible, each component is contractible with exactly one source.
- **claim-residue-sublevels** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-residue-sublevels)): For every nonempty spherical $T$ and the data just specified:* 1. *$\mathcal R_{<}(T,A_Tx;q)$ is empty or contractible;* 2. *whenever the additional exterior hypotheses hold, $\mathcal R_{\le}(T,A_Tx;q,I,c)$ is nonempty and contractible.* *For $T=\varnothing$, the strict sublevel is empty, and the exterior hypotheses for the special closed sublevel cannot hold.
  - Explanation: Strict sublevels are empty or contractible and special closed sublevels are nonempty and contractible.
- **claim-global-well-order** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-global-well-order)): Definition <a href="#def:global-sign-height" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:global-sign-height">45</a> gives a well-order of the vertices of $\mathcal D$. Distinct comparable cosets have different block sequences, so the final tie refinement does not affect either of their earlier links.
  - Explanation: The sign-height definition well-orders the coset vertices; comparable cosets never fully tie.
- **claim-upper-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-global-upper-comparison)): Every earlier upper extension of $P$ adds an active color. Every spherical upper extension adding a nonempty set of active colors and no other colors is earlier than $P$.
  - Explanation: Earlier upper extensions add an active color; extensions adding only active colors are earlier.
- **claim-active-spherical** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-global-active-spherical)): The subset $T\cup\operatorname{Act}(P)$ is spherical.
  - Explanation: The type together with its active colors is spherical.
- **claim-upper-link** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-global-upper-link)): The earlier strict upper part of $P$ is empty or contractible.
  - Explanation: The earlier strict upper part is empty or contractible via retraction onto active-color extensions.
- **claim-lower-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-global-lower-comparison)): Every earlier lower coset has an eligible open color. A lower coset all of whose open colors are eligible is earlier than $P$. The earlier lower part retracts by an order map pointwise above the identity onto the poset of these all-eligible lower cosets.
  - Explanation: Earlier lower cosets have an eligible color and retract onto the all-eligible poset.
- **claim-residue-blocks** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-global-residue-blocks)): The all-eligible lower poset at $P=A_Tx$ is a product of the residue sublevel posets of Proposition <a href="#prop:residue-sublevels" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:residue-sublevels">44</a>, each with a largest element adjoined, with the all-largest tuple deleted. More precisely, its factors are as follows.* 1. *Each connected component $B$ of $T$ with negative sign at its highest level $q_B$ contributes the strict sublevel in the coset $A_Bx$ at level $q_B$.* 2. *For a positive sign, group all components of $T$ belonging to the same positive-support component $I$ at the same level $q$. Their union is $B=T\cap I$, and it contributes the special closed sublevel in $A_Bx$ with data $I,q,c_I=p_I^q(P)$.* *Every strict factor is empty or contractible, and every special closed factor is nonempty and contractible.
  - Explanation: The all-eligible lower poset is a product of residue sublevel posets with a top adjoined, minus the all-top tuple.
- **claim-product-minus-top** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-global-product-minus-top)): Let $\mathcal E_1,\ldots,\mathcal E_r$ be posets whose realizations are each empty or contractible, and let $\mathcal E_a^+$ denote the result of adjoining a largest element. Then $$\left(\prod_{a=1}^r\mathcal E_a^+\right) \setminus\{(\top,\ldots,\top)\}$$ is empty if every $\mathcal E_a$ is empty, and otherwise has contractible realization. The empty product has the same empty conclusion after its largest element is deleted.
  - Explanation: A product of augmented empty-or-contractible posets minus its top is empty or contractible.
- **claim-lower-link** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-global-lower-link)): The earlier strict lower part of every $P\in\mathcal D$ is empty or contractible.
  - Explanation: The earlier strict lower part of every vertex is empty or contractible.
- **claim-deligne-contractible** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-deligne-contractible)): For every Coxeter matrix on a finite set $S$, the order complex of the spherical-coset poset $\mathcal D$ is contractible.
  - Explanation: The order complex of the spherical-coset poset is contractible for every finite Coxeter matrix.
- **claim-artin-structural** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-artin-structural)): Let $A=A(W,S)$ be an Artin group with $S$ finite, let $A^+$ be its positive Artin monoid, and let $k$ be the number of irreducible spherical components of the Coxeter diagram of $(W,S)$. Then:* 1. *$A$ is torsion-free.* 2. *The center satisfies $Z(A)\cong\mathbb Z^k$.* 3. *The natural homomorphism $\eta\colon A^+\to A$ induces a homotopy equivalence $$B\eta\colon BA^+\longrightarrow BA.$$
  - Explanation: Finite-rank Artin groups are torsion-free, have center of rank given by spherical components, and monoid and group classifying spaces agree.
- **claim-boyd-stability** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-cor-boyd-stability)): Let $A_1$ be the Artin group of a Coxeter matrix $m_1$ on a finite set $\Sigma_1$, and choose a vertex $\sigma_1\in\Sigma_1$. For each $n\ge2$, adjoin new vertices $\sigma_2,\ldots,\sigma_n$ and let $A_n$ be the Artin group of the Coxeter matrix $m_n$ on $\Sigma_n=\Sigma_1\sqcup\{\sigma_2,\ldots,\sigma_n\}$. Retain $m_1$ on $\Sigma_1$, put diagonal entries equal to $1$, and set the remaining off-diagonal entries, symmetrically, by $$\begin{aligned} m_n(\sigma_j,\sigma_{j+1})&=3 &&(1\le j<n),\\ m_n(\sigma_j,\sigma_\ell)&=2 &&(1\le j<\ell\le n,\ \ell-j\ge2),\\ m_n(u,\sigma_j)&=2 &&(u\in\Sigma_1\setminus\{\sigma_1\},\ 2\le j\le n). \end{aligned}$$ Let $s_n\colon A_{n-1}\hookrightarrow A_n$ be the natural inclusion for $n\ge2$. For every $n\ge2$, every abelian group $C$, used as a constant coefficient group, and every integer $i\ge0$, the induced map $$(Bs_n)_*\colon H_i(BA_{n-1};C)\longrightarrow H_i(BA_n;C)$$ is an isomorphism when $i<n/2$ and a surjection when $i=n/2$.
  - Explanation: For fixed-core braid-tail families homology with constant coefficients stabilizes in the stated range.

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-finite-unfolding",
        "exact_quote": "If $J\\subseteq S$ is spherical, then $\\widetilde C_{\\widetilde J}$ is positive definite and simply laced, and defines a finite Coxeter system. Replacing each original letter $s_i$, $i\\in J$, by the product of the commuting reflections in all vertices of its color gives a lift of words to this system.       "explanation": "For spherical J the unfolded Cartan matrix is positive definite and simply laced; lifts preserve descents and reducedness.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-twist-action",
        "exact_quote": "For each original color $i$, let $$B_i=\\prod_{\\alpha\\in\\widetilde{\\{i\\}}}B_\\alpha,\n \\qquad\n D_i=\\prod_{\\alpha\\in\\widetilde{\\{i\\}}}D_\\alpha.$$ These products are independent of their order up to bimodule homotopy. The assignments $\\sigma_i\\mapsto B_i$ and $\\sigma_i^{-1}\\mapsto D_i$ define an action of the original Artin group by invertible bimodule homotopy classes. For $x\\in A$ write $B_x$ for a representing complex, and write $F_i=B_i\\otimes_Z-$ on left complexes.*\n\n*For every $H\\in\\mathscr C$, right tensoring of labels, $$T_H(P_{s,X})=P_{s,X\\otimes H},$$ is an additive functor on projectives that preserves path lengths and extends to their bounded homotopy category. It commutes naturally up to homotopy with the positive and inverse color twists, and hence with each $B_x\\otimes_Z-$. All the action identities above are bimodule chain homotopy equivalences, so they remain valid upon tensoring with finite complexes of modules that are not projective.",

      },

It commutes naturally up to homotopy with the positive and inverse color twists, and hence with each $B_x\\otimes_Z-$.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-layer-structure",
        "exact_quote": "The full subcategories defined by minimal generator levels $d\\leq0$ and $d\\geq0$ form a bounded $t$-structure. Its truncation $\\tau_{\\leq k}E$ is represented by the subcomplex of a minimal model on levels at most $k$; the complementary quotient represents $\\tau_{\\geq k+1}E$.*\n\n*The heart is equivalent to the abelian category of finite graded coefficient arrays just described. In particular, subobjects of a pure layer, after shifting that layer into the heart, are precisely graded subrepresentations of its array. The unshifted cohomology layer at $d$ is $Y^d(E)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-254.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

        "exact_quote": "Suppose that an exact functor $F$ has layer amplitude $[0,1]$.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-layers-extremal-tests",
        "exact_quote": "For a pure layer $M$ at level $d$, $$H^d(e_\\alpha M)\\cong\\operatorname{soc}_\\alpha M,\n \\qquad\n H^{d+2}(e_\\alpha M)\\cong\n                  (\\operatorname{top}_\\alpha M)\\langle2\\rangle,\n \\label{eq:layers-extremal-tests}$$ as graded multiplicity spaces, with their corresponding placements. The shift on the top is the internal placement of the token. If a minimal complex $E$ has no generators above $d$ at $\\alpha$ or its neighbors, its test in degree $d+2$ is still $(\\operatorname{top}_\\alpha Y^d(E))\\langle2\\rangle$, and all higher test cohomology there vanishes. If it has no generators below $d$ at those vertices, its test in degree $d$ is the socle of $Y^d(E)$ at $\\alpha$.*\n\n*A nonzero pure layer supported on a vertex set has a nonzero top and a nonzero socle somewhere in that set.",
        "source_id": "PM-254.1"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-positive-layer",
        "exact_quote": "Each $F_i$ has layer amplitude $[0,1]$. Its upper piece on a pure layer $M$ at $d$ is supported on the fiber of $i$, has no arrows, and has multiplicity $\\dim\\operatorname{top}_\\alpha M$ at a vertex $\\alpha$ of that fiber. Its bottom piece has unchanged multiplicities at all other vertices, and at $\\alpha$ in the fiber it has multiplicity $$\\dim Y^d(F_iM)_\\alpha\n =\\sum_\\beta(2\\mathop{\\mathrm{id}}-\\widetilde C)_{\\alpha\\beta}\n       \\dim M_\\beta\n       -\\dim M_\\alpha+\\dim\\operatorname{top}_\\alpha M.\n \\label{eq:layers-positive-dimension}$$ The bottom piece has zero socle in color $i$.       "exact_statement": "Each $F_i$ has layer amplitude $[0,1]$.         "exact_quote": "For a positive unfolded letter $\\alpha$, if $h_J(X)$ is finite, $$h_J(X)\\leq h_J(B_\\alpha X)\\leq h_J(X)+1.$$ An increase occurs exactly when $\\alpha$ is an old highest-test vertex; in that case it is the only new highest-test vertex. Vanishing of all tests persists. The same assertions hold for right actions.",
        "source_id": "PM-254.1"

        "exact_quote": "Let $b$ be a positive word in the unfolded finite-type Artin generators.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-height-data-tensor-truncation",
        "exact_quote": "For $H\\in\\mathscr C$, right tensoring of all projective labels by $H$ defines a $t$-exact functor $T_H$ for the layer $t$-structure. It commutes naturally with its truncations, layers, and all the color twist functors and their inverses.*\n\n*In particular, if a subobject is constructed by applying a word of twists, taking a layer truncation, and applying the inverse word, this construction commutes with $T_H$ as a morphism into the ambient object. No choice of minimal model or Gaussian elimination is required to be natural.",
        "source_id": "PM-254.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-family-proportionality",
        "exact_quote": "There are nonnegative vectors $m^d(x)\\in\\mathbb R^{\\widehat S}$ such that the weighted generator dimension vector of the layer family is $$\\sum_L w_L\\dim Y_L^d(x)=\\phi m^d(x).\n \\label{eq:height-data-proportionality}$$ The weighted top and socle dimension vectors of these layers also have the form $\\phi v$. Consequently, if a top or socle occurs anywhere in a color in the family, then at every unfolded vertex of that color it occurs in at least one member of the family.*\n\n\n\n*For each $x$ the vectors $m^d(x)$ have finite support in $d$. Uniformly over $x$, $d$, and the colors, each coordinate has only finitely many possible values in a bounded interval. Operations on the left by $\\sigma_i^{\\pm1}$ change $m^d$ only in coordinate $i$, and $$m_o^d(x)=\n \\begin{cases}1,&d=0,\\\\0,&d\\ne0.\\end{cases}\n \\label{eq:height-data-frame-coordinate}$$ On folded signed counts, either twist in color $i$ acts by the coordinate reflection for $C$: $$\\sum_d(-1)^d m^d(\\sigma_i^{\\pm1}x)\n   =s_i\\left(\\sum_d(-1)^d m^d(x)\\right).\n \\label{eq:height-data-signed-reflection}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-254.1"
      },

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-family-proportionality",

Consequently, if a top or socle occurs anywhere in a color in the family, then at every unfolded vertex of that color it occurs in at least one member of the family.*\n\n\n\n*For each $x$ the vectors $m^d(x)$ have finite support in $d$.         "anchor": "stmt-prop-convex-subobjects",
        "exact_quote": "Let $J\\subseteq S$ be spherical, and suppose that at $x$ the data are capped at $q$ on $J\\cup\\partial J$. Let $p$ agree with $m^q(x)$ outside $J$ and be harmonic on $J$, and put $e=m^q(x)-p$, which is supported on $J$. Assume that for *every* representative $z\\in A_Jx$ whose data are capped at $q$ on $J\\cup\\partial J$, $$m_j^q(z)\\geq p_j\\qquad(j\\in J).\n \\label{eq:height-data-universal-cap-inequality}$$ Then $e_J$ belongs to the convex hull in $\\mathbb R^J$ of vectors $s_J$ with the following property: $s$ is zero outside $J$, and there are graded subrepresentations $$\\mathcal S_L\\subseteq Y_L^q(x),\\qquad\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\mathcal S_L\\subseteq\\widetilde J,\n \\qquad\n \\sum_Lw_L\\dim\\mathcal S_L=\\phi s.\n \\label{eq:height-data-convex-subrepresentations}$$ One may use a finite collection of such vectors, together with the zero vector.*\n\n*If all inequalities in <a href=\"#eq:height-data-universal-cap-inequality\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:height-data-universal-cap-inequality\">[eq:height-data-universal-cap-inequality]</a> are strict, $e_J$ is in the ordinary interior in $\\mathbb R^J$ of such a convex hull.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-right-duality",
        "exact_quote": "For every $h\\in A_T$, every $\\ell,\\beta\\in\\widetilde T$, and every integer $r$, $$\\label{eq:two-point-duality}\n K^r_{\\ell\\beta}(h)\n   =K^{-2-r}_{\\beta\\ell}(h^{-1}).$$ If $J,J'\\subseteq T$, the rows with indices outside $\\widetilde J'$ are unchanged by left multiplication of $h$ by $A_{J'}$, and the columns with indices outside $\\widetilde J$ are unchanged by right multiplication of $h$ by $A_J$.",
        "source_id": "PM-254.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-right-duality",
          "exact_quote": "For every $h\\in A_T$, every $\\ell,\\beta\\in\\widetilde T$, and every integer $r$, $$\\label{eq:two-point-duality}\n K^r_{\\ell\\beta}(h)\n   =K^{-2-r}_{\\beta\\ell}(h^{-1}).$$ If $J,J'\\subseteq T$, the rows with indices outside $\\widetilde J'$ are unchanged by left multiplication of $h$ by $A_{J'}$, and the columns with indices outside $\\widetilde J$ are unchanged by right multiplication of $h$ by $A_J$.",
          "source_id": "PM-254.1"

        "exact_quote": "For nonidentity simple elements $d_1,\\ldots,d_k$, the factorization $d_1\\cdots d_k$ is obtained by repeatedly extracting the greatest simple suffix if and only if $$\\label{eq:two-point-greedy-condition}\n D_R(d_i)\\subseteq D_L(d_{i+1})\\qquad(1\\le i<k).$$ Here the descent sets are those of the corresponding elements of the Coxeter group.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-isolated-layer",
        "exact_quote": "Let $I\\subseteq S$ be nonempty and connected. Let $c_I$ be a strictly positive vector with $C_Ic_I=0$, and extend $c$ by zero on $\\widehat S\\setminus I$. Let $J\\subseteq I$ be spherical, possibly empty, let $q>0$ be an integer, and fix a coset $A_Jx$. Suppose that the fixed data on $(I\\setminus J)\\cup\\partial I$ are capped at $q$ and satisfy $$m^q|_{(I\\setminus J)\\cup\\partial I}\n   =c|_{(I\\setminus J)\\cup\\partial I}.\n \\label{eq:rigidity-exterior-data}$$ It is impossible that every representative $z\\in A_Jx$ capped at $q$ on $I$ satisfies $$m_j^q(z)>c_j\\qquad\\text{for every }j\\in J.\n \\label{eq:rigidity-strict-hypothesis}$$ Equivalently, if $J\\ne\\varnothing$ and <a href=\"#eq:rigidity-exterior-data\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:rigidity-exterior-data\">[eq:rigidity-exterior-data]</a> holds, there is a representative capped on $I$ and a color $j\\in J$ with $m_j^q\\le c_j$. If $J=\\varnothing$, the exterior situation <a href=\"#eq:rigidity-exterior-data\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:rigidity-exterior-data\">[eq:rigidity-exterior-data]</a> itself is impossible.",
        "source_id": "PM-254.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-isolated-layer",
          "exact_quote": "Let $I\\subseteq S$ be nonempty and connected. Let $c_I$ be a strictly positive vector with $C_Ic_I=0$, and extend $c$ by zero on $\\widehat S\\setminus I$. Let $J\\subseteq I$ be spherical, possibly empty, let $q>0$ be an integer, and fix a coset $A_Jx$. Suppose that the fixed data on $(I\\setminus J)\\cup\\partial I$ are capped at $q$ and satisfy $$m^q|_{(I\\setminus J)\\cup\\partial I}\n   =c|_{(I\\setminus J)\\cup\\partial I}.\n \\label{eq:rigidity-exterior-data}$$ It is impossible that every representative $z\\in A_Jx$ capped at $q$ on $I$ satisfies $$m_j^q(z)>c_j\\qquad\\text{for every }j\\in J.\n \\label{eq:rigidity-strict-hypothesis}$$ Equivalently, if $J\\ne\\varnothing$ and <a href=\"#eq:rigidity-exterior-data\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:rigidity-exterior-data\">[eq:rigidity-exterior-data]</a> holds, there is a representative capped on $I$ and a color $j\\in J$ with $m_j^q\\le c_j$. If $J=\\varnothing$, the exterior situation <a href=\"#eq:rigidity-exterior-data\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:rigidity-exterior-data\">[eq:rigidity-exterior-data]</a> itself is impossible.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-rigidity-hom-filtration",
        "exact_quote": "For pure projective layers $P,Y$, including those formed from graded subrepresentations, there are perfect pairings $$\\mathsf H_i^{n,v}(P,Y)\\ \\times\\\n \\mathsf H_{2-i}^{-n,\\,2-v}(Y,P)\\longrightarrow\\mathbb C.$$ Consequently, $$\\begin{split}\n h_2(P,Y)&=h_0(Y,P),\\\\\n (\\dim P)^{\\mathsf t}\\widetilde C\\,\\dim Y\n   &=h_0(P,Y)+h_0(Y,P)-h_1(P,Y).\n \\end{split}\n \\label{eq:rigidity-euler}$$ Here $\\dim P$ and $\\dim Y$ count projective generators, with all placements included. For any two labels $L,H$ and any levels $l,k$, $$h_1(Y_L^l,Y_H^k)=0.\n \\label{eq:rigidity-middle-vanishing}$$ The same vanishing holds after replacing either layer by a direct summand as a pure layer.",
        "source_id": "PM-254.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-rigidity-hom-filtration",
          "exact_quote": "For pure projective layers $P,Y$, including those formed from graded subrepresentations, there are perfect pairings $$\\mathsf H_i^{n,v}(P,Y)\\ \\times\\\n \\mathsf H_{2-i}^{-n,\\,2-v}(Y,P)\\longrightarrow\\mathbb C.$$ Consequently, $$\\begin{split}\n h_2(P,Y)&=h_0(Y,P),\\\\\n (\\dim P)^{\\mathsf t}\\widetilde C\\,\\dim Y\n   &=h_0(P,Y)+h_0(Y,P)-h_1(P,Y).\n \\end{split}\n \\label{eq:rigidity-euler}$$ Here $\\dim P$ and $\\dim Y$ count projective generators, with all placements included. For any two labels $L,H$ and any levels $l,k$, $$h_1(Y_L^l,Y_H^k)=0.\n \\label{eq:rigidity-middle-vanishing}$$ The same vanishing holds after replacing either layer by a direct summand as a pure layer.",
          "source_id": "PM-254.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For pure projective layers $P,Y$, including those formed from graded subrepresentations, there are perfect pairings $$\\mathsf H_i^{n,v}(P,Y)\\ \\times\\\n \\mathsf H_{2-i}^{-n,\\,2-v}(Y,P)\\longrightarrow\\mathbb C.$$ Consequently, $$\\begin{split}\n h_2(P,Y)&=h_0(Y,P),\\\\\n (\\dim P)^{\\mathsf t}\\widetilde C\\,\\dim Y\n   &=h_0(P,Y)+h_0(Y,P)-h_1(P,Y).\n \\end{split}\n \\label{eq:rigidity-euler}$$ Here $\\dim P$ and $\\dim Y$ count projective generators, with all placements included. For any two labels $L,H$ and any levels $l,k$, $$h_1(Y_L^l,Y_H^k)=0.\n \\label{eq:rigidity-middle-vanishing}$$ The same vanishing holds after replacing either layer by a direct summand as a pure layer.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-rigidity-secondary-injection",
        "exact_quote": "Let $I\\subseteq S$, and suppose the family $E_L$ is capped at $q$ on $I$. For every $L$, let $M_L$ be a direct summand of the pure layer $Y_L^q$ supported on $\\widetilde I$. If $d<q$, then for every pair of labels $L,H$ there are injections $$\\mathsf H_0^{n,v}(M_L,Y_H^d)\n   \\lhook\\joinrel\\longrightarrow\n \\mathsf H_2^{n+1,v}(M_L,Y_H^{d-1}),\n \\qquad n+v=d-q.\n \\label{eq:rigidity-secondary-injection}$$ In particular, for $\\mathcal M=(M_L)_L$ and $\\mathcal Y^d=(Y_H^d)_H$, $$h_0^{\\mathrm{wt}}(\\mathcal M,\\mathcal Y^d)\n \\le h_0^{\\mathrm{wt}}(\\mathcal Y^{d-1},\\mathcal M).\n \\label{eq:rigidity-secondary-bound}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-254.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-rigidity-secondary-injection",
          "exact_quote": "Let $I\\subseteq S$, and suppose the family $E_L$ is capped at $q$ on $I$. For every $L$, let $M_L$ be a direct summand of the pure layer $Y_L^q$ supported on $\\widetilde I$. If $d<q$, then for every pair of labels $L,H$ there are injections $$\\mathsf H_0^{n,v}(M_L,Y_H^d)\n   \\lhook\\joinrel\\longrightarrow\n \\mathsf H_2^{n+1,v}(M_L,Y_H^{d-1}),\n \\qquad n+v=d-q.\n \\label{eq:rigidity-secondary-injection}$$ In particular, for $\\mathcal M=(M_L)_L$ and $\\mathcal Y^d=(Y_H^d)_H$, $$h_0^{\\mathrm{wt}}(\\mathcal M,\\mathcal Y^d)\n \\le h_0^{\\mathrm{wt}}(\\mathcal Y^{d-1},\\mathcal M).\n \\label{eq:rigidity-secondary-bound}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-254.1"
        }
      ],

        "exact_quote": "Let $I\\subseteq S$ be nonempty and connected, and let $c$ be zero off $I$ and strictly positive on $I$, with $C_Ic_I=0$. Let $J\\subseteq I$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-filtration-principle",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal P$ be a set-sized poset, and well-order its elements by $\\prec$. For $v\\in\\mathcal P$, define the earlier lower and upper posets $$\\mathcal L_v=\\{w\\in\\mathcal P:w<v,\\ w\\prec v\\},\\qquad\n \\mathcal U_v=\\{w\\in\\mathcal P:v<w,\\ w\\prec v\\}.$$ The link along which $v$ is added to the induced order complex on its predecessors is the simplicial join of the order complexes of $\\mathcal L_v$ and $\\mathcal U_v$. If one poset is empty, this link is the order complex of the other; if both are empty, the link is empty.*\n\n*Suppose each attaching link is empty or contractible. Then every component of $|\\mathcal P|$ is contractible and contains exactly one *source*, meaning a vertex with empty attaching link. In particular, the conclusion holds if each of $\\mathcal L_v$ and $\\mathcal U_v$ is empty or contractible for every $v$.",
        "source_id": "PM-254.1"
      },

        "exact_quote": "The all-eligible lower poset at $P=A_Tx$ is a product of the residue sublevel posets of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:residue-sublevels\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:residue-sublevels\">44</a>, each with a largest element adjoined, with the all-largest tuple deleted. For each $n\\ge2$, adjoin new vertices $\\sigma_2,\\ldots,\\sigma_n$ and let $A_n$ be the Artin group of the Coxeter matrix $m_n$ on $\\Sigma_n=\\Sigma_1\\sqcup\\{\\sigma_2,\\ldots,\\sigma_n\\}$. Retain $m_1$ on $\\Sigma_1$, put diagonal entries equal to $1$, and set the remaining off-diagonal entries, symmetrically, by $$\\begin{aligned}\n m_n(\\sigma_j,\\sigma_{j+1})&=3\n     &&(1\\le j<n),\\\\\n m_n(\\sigma_j,\\sigma_\\ell)&=2\n     &&(1\\le j<\\ell\\le n,\\ \\ell-j\\ge2),\\\\\n m_n(u,\\sigma_j)&=2\n     &&(u\\in\\Sigma_1\\setminus\\{\\sigma_1\\},\\ 2\\le j\\le n).\n\\end{aligned}$$ Let $s_n\\colon A_{n-1}\\hookrightarrow A_n$ be the natural inclusion for $n\\ge2$. For every $n\\ge2$, every abelian group $C$, used as a constant coefficient group, and every integer $i\\ge0$, the induced map $$(Bs_n)_*\\colon H_i(BA_{n-1};C)\\longrightarrow H_i(BA_n;C)$$ is an isomorphism when $i<n/2$ and a surjection when $i=n/2$.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **spherical subset** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A subset $T\subseteq S$ is *spherical* if its standard Coxeter parabolic $W_T$ is finite.
- **Salvetti complex** ([source](#sec:introduction)): The standard Artin Salvetti complex $X(W,S)$ has one cell of dimension $\|T\|$ for each spherical subset $T$, including its vertex for $T=\varnothing$.
- **order complex realization** ([source](#sec:topology)): For a poset $\mathcal P$, its realization $\|\mathcal P\|$ will always mean the order complex: vertices are elements of $\mathcal P$, simplices are nonempty finite chains, and the realization has its CW topology.
- **right-divisibility** ([source](#pm-h-7)): An element $b$ *right-divides* $a$ if $a=cb$ for some positive element $c$; left divisibility is defined in the opposite order.
- **spherical-coset poset** ([source](#pm-h-4)): Write $\mathcal D$ for the poset of spherical right cosets $A_Tx$, ordered by inclusion with nested types.
- **unfolded vertex** ([source](#pm-h-14)): An unfolded vertex is a pair $\alpha=(s,L)$ with $L$ simple.
- **minimal complex** ([source](#pm-h-18)): A bounded projective complex is *minimal* if each differential entry belongs to the graded radical of the additive category of projectives.
- **cap** ([source](#stmt-def-height-data-cap)): For $q\in\mathbb Z$ and $K\subseteq\widehat S$, the data at a representative $x$ are *capped at $q$ on $K$* if $$m_i^d(x)=0\qquad(i\in K, d>q).$$ For a set of colors $J$, let $\partial J$ denote its outside neighbors in the framed diagram; it includes the frame when the frame is a neighbor. Positivity of the family weights makes a cap equivalent to the absence of generators in all the corresponding unfolded vertices and levels in every member of the family.
- **candidate** ([source](#stmt-def-two-point-candidate)): A *candidate* in $A_Tx_0$ is a pair $$X=(J,A_Jz),\qquad J\subsetneq T,\qquad z\in A_Tx_0,$$ with the following properties. Put $V=T\setminus J$, and let $p$ agree with $m^q(z)$ outside $J$ and be harmonic on $J$. 1. The data are capped at $q$ on $V$. 2. The vector $a$, defined on $T$ by $a_i=-(Cp)_i$, is strictly positive on $V$ and zero on $J$. 3. For every representative $y\in A_Jz$ whose data are capped at $q$ on $T$, one has $$m_j^q(y)\ge p_j\qquad(j\in J).$$
- **global sign height** ([source](#stmt-def-global-sign-height)): For $P=A_Tx$, $k\ge0$, and $i\in\widehat S$, define a sign $\eta_i^k(P)$ as follows. It is zero if $p_i^k(P)=0$. If $p_i^k(P)>0$, let $I$ be its positive-support component at level $k$. Set $\eta_i^k(P)=+1$ precisely when $$\label{eq:global-null-sign} k>0,\qquad I\subseteq S,\qquad \bigl(Cp^k(P)\bigr)_I=0,\qquad p_j^d(P)=0 \quad(j\in I\cup\partial I,\ d>k).$$ In every other positive-coordinate case set $\eta_i^k(P)=-1$. Define the level block $$\label{eq:global-height-block} \mathsf b_k(P)= \left( \sum_{i\in\widehat S}p_i^k(P),\ (p_i^k(P))_{i\in\widehat S},\ \sum_{i\in T}\eta_i^k(P) \right).$$ Within a block use lexicographic order: first the sum, then the vector in the fixed coordinate order, then the integer. Compare the finite-support sequences $(\mathsf b_k(P))_{k\ge0}$ at their highest differing level, with the smaller block earlier. Finally, choose a well-order of each set of cosets with identical block sequences.
- **eligible color** ([source](#stmt-def-global-eligible)): For a lower coset $Q=A_Rz\subsetneq P$, call an open color $i\in T\setminus R$ *eligible* if $$\label{eq:global-eligibility} \begin{gathered} m_i^d(z)=0\quad\text{for every }d>q_i,\\ m_i^{q_i}(z)<p_i^{q_i}(P), \quad\text{or}\quad m_i^{q_i}(z)=p_i^{q_i}(P) \ \text{ and }\ \eta_i^{q_i}(P)=+1. \end{gathered}$$ The first line is required in both alternatives of the second line. Write $E(Q)$ for the eligible open colors. This definition is independent of $z$, since all its tested colors lie outside $R$.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-higher-homotopy-vanishing | higher homotopy groups of the Salvetti complex | = | $0$ | degree at least two |
| q-frame-edge-weight | frame edge weight | = | $1$ | original vertices |
| q-finite-edge-weight | diagram weight for a finite label | = | $2\cos(\pi/m_{ij})$ | finite labels |
| q-infinite-edge-weight | diagram weight for an infinite label | = | $2$ | infinite labels |
| q-cartan-diagonal | diagonal entry of the Cartan matrix | = | $2$ | all vertices |
| q-fusion-hom-bound | dimension of Hom from simple to edge object tensor simple | ≤ | $1$ | finite edges |
| q-edge-object-dimension | dimension of the edge object | = | $b_{st}$ | ordered edges |
| q-circle-value | Temperley-Lieb circle value | = | $2\cos(\pi/m)$ | labels at least four |
| q-trace-degree | degree of the Frobenius trace | = | $-2$ | whole algebra |
| q-token-trace | trace of a length-two token | = | $w_L$ | unfolded vertices |
| q-generator-level | level of a generator | = | $c+u$ | all generators |
| q-positive-amplitude | layer amplitude of a positive color twist | = | $[0,1]$ | each color |
| q-inverse-amplitude | layer amplitude of the inverse | = | $[-1,0]$ | exact equivalences |
| q-height-increase | test height after a positive letter | ≤ | $h_J(X)+1$ | finite test height |
| q-frame-coordinate | frame coordinate at level zero | = | $1$ | level zero |
| q-D0 | normalization constant | = | $\sum_Lw_L^2$ | simple labels |
| q-normalized-level-bound | normalized level bound | ≥ | $-1$ | single simple |
| q-duality-offset | right generator count of the inverse | = | $K^{-2-r}_{\beta\ell}(h^{-1})$ | all degrees |
| q-boundary-level | lowest boundary-meeting layer | ≤ | $0$ | fixed coset |
| q-residue-height | height of a residue sublevel pair | ≤ | $0$ | pairs in the sublevel |
| q-center-rank | center of the Artin group | = | $\mathbb Z^k$ | k spherical components |
| q-stability-iso | homological degree of isomorphism | < | $n/2$ | constant coefficients |
| q-stability-surj | homological degree of surjection | = | $n/2$ | constant coefficients |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **s-parabolic-embedding** [claim](#stmt-thm-topology-parabolic-embedding) / [proof](#stmt-thm-topology-parabolic-embedding): cited classical theorem -> claim-parabolic-embedding
- **s-finite-monoid** [claim](#stmt-thm-topology-finite-monoid) / [proof](#stmt-thm-topology-finite-monoid): cited finite-type monoid results -> claim-finite-monoid
- **s-spherical-asphericity** [claim](#stmt-thm-topology-spherical-asphericity) / [proof](#stmt-thm-topology-spherical-asphericity): cited spherical theorem -> claim-spherical-asphericity
- **s-salvetti-comparison** [claim](#stmt-lem-salvetti-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-salvetti-comparison): Quillen Theorem A on the face poset -> equivalence with the coset poset
- **s-cartan-inverse** [claim](#stmt-lem-cartan-positive-null) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cartan-positive-null): Perron-Frobenius series and scaled quadratic form -> nonnegative harmonic extension
- **s-fusion-category** [claim](#stmt-prop-fusion-construction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-fusion-construction): Temperley-Lieb quotient by negligible maps -> spherical fusion category
- **s-frobenius-trace** [claim](#stmt-lem-category-frobenius) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-category-frobenius): pairing and pivotal symmetry -> Frobenius trace
- **s-finite-unfolding** [claim](#stmt-lem-finite-unfolding) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-finite-unfolding): scaled form on unfolded matrix -> finite simply laced unfolding
- **s-twist-inverse** [claim](#stmt-lem-category-twist-inverse) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-category-twist-inverse): Gaussian cancellation -> inverse twists
- **s-local-braid** [claim](#stmt-lem-category-local-braid) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-category-local-braid): conjugation of evaluation twists -> local braid relations
- **s-twist-action** [claim](#stmt-prop-twist-action) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-twist-action): Matsumoto moves on unfolded reduced words -> Artin action
- **s-layer-structure** [claim](#stmt-lem-layer-structure) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-layer-structure): level truncations -> t-structure
- **s-adjacent-layers** [claim](#stmt-lem-layers-amplitude-extension) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-layers-amplitude-extension): truncation triangles -> adjacent-layer sequences
- **s-bottom-composition** [claim](#stmt-lem-layers-bottom-composition) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-layers-bottom-composition): long exact sequence -> bottom layer calculus
- **s-extremal-tests** [claim](#stmt-lem-layers-extremal-tests) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-layers-extremal-tests): path length counting -> top and socle tests
- **s-positive-layer** [claim](#stmt-lem-positive-layer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-positive-layer): cone cancellation and signed counts -> positive amplitude
- **s-inverse-amplitude** [claim](#stmt-lem-layers-inverse-amplitude) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-layers-inverse-amplitude): orthogonality -> inverse amplitude
- **s-height-change** [claim](#stmt-lem-layers-height-change) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-layers-height-change): evaluation triangle -> height monotonicity
- **s-top-divisor** [claim](#stmt-lem-top-divisor) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-top-divisor): induction on word length -> divisor test
- **s-reduced-amplitude** [claim](#stmt-cor-reduced-amplitude) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-reduced-amplitude): single height increase for reduced words -> reduced-word amplitude
- **s-tensor-truncation** [claim](#stmt-lem-height-data-tensor-truncation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-height-data-tensor-truncation): naturality of label tensoring -> t-exact tensoring
- **s-family-proportionality** [claim](#stmt-lem-family-proportionality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-family-proportionality): weighted fusion identity -> folded height vectors
- **s-capped-layer** [claim](#stmt-lem-height-data-capped-layer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-height-data-capped-layer): truncation triangle -> cap preservation
- **s-cap-accessibility** [claim](#stmt-lem-cap-accessibility) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cap-accessibility): top removal along reduced expressions -> jointly capped representative
- **s-convex-subobjects** [claim](#stmt-prop-convex-subobjects) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-convex-subobjects): reflection words, truncations and chamber covering -> convex hull of subrepresentations
- **s-right-duality** [claim](#stmt-lem-right-duality) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-right-duality): adjoint duality of minimal complexes -> degree reversal
- **s-negative-bound** [claim](#stmt-lem-two-point-negative-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-two-point-negative-bound): simultaneous cancellation -> level bound
- **s-tensor-filtration** [claim](#stmt-lem-two-point-tensor-filtration) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-two-point-tensor-filtration): length filtration -> top exclusion
- **s-remove-tops** [claim](#stmt-lem-two-point-remove-tops) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-two-point-remove-tops): cap-preserving positive moves -> representative without right tops
- **s-subrep-tensor** [claim](#stmt-lem-two-point-subrepresentation-tensor) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-two-point-subrepresentation-tensor): coefficient substitution -> subfamily vanishing
- **s-euler-estimate** [claim](#stmt-lem-two-point-euler-estimate) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-two-point-euler-estimate): Euler characteristic and convexity -> middle cohomology bound
- **s-greedy** [claim](#stmt-lem-two-point-greedy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-two-point-greedy): descent-set induction -> greedy factorization
- **s-parabolic-detection** [claim](#stmt-lem-parabolic-detection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-parabolic-detection): successive common-divisor removal -> parabolic detector
- **s-candidate-uniqueness** [claim](#stmt-prop-candidate-uniqueness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-parabolic-detection-2): opposite two-point inequalities against a positive definite form -> uniqueness of candidates
- **s-hom-pairing** [claim](#stmt-lem-rigidity-hom-filtration) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rigidity-hom-filtration): supertrace pairing and column filtration -> Hom Euler identity
- **s-secondary-injection** [claim](#stmt-lem-rigidity-secondary-injection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rigidity-secondary-injection): secondary differential under cap -> Hom injection
- **s-boundary-separation** [claim](#stmt-lem-rigidity-separation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rigidity-separation): affine inequality over the convex hull -> boundary vanishing
- **s-isolated-layer** [claim](#stmt-prop-isolated-layer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rigidity-separation-2): socle-removal induction to the frame layer -> isolated layer obstruction
- **s-vertex-filtration** [claim](#stmt-lem-filtration-principle) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-filtration-principle): transfinite cone attachment -> component contractibility
- **s-residue-sublevels** [claim](#stmt-prop-residue-sublevels) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-residue-sublevels): induction on type size with height filtration -> sublevel contractibility
- **s-global-well-order** [claim](#stmt-lem-global-well-order) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-global-well-order): finiteness of blocks and ordinal ranking -> well-order of vertices
- **s-upper-comparison** [claim](#stmt-lem-global-upper-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-global-upper-comparison): harmonic comparison formula -> active colors
- **s-active-spherical** [claim](#stmt-lem-global-active-spherical) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-global-active-spherical): positive definite or null dichotomy -> spherical active union
- **s-upper-link** [claim](#stmt-prop-global-upper-link) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-global-upper-link): order retraction to a poset with a maximum -> contractible upper part
- **s-lower-comparison** [claim](#stmt-lem-global-lower-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-global-lower-comparison): closure retraction onto all-eligible cosets -> eligible lower poset
- **s-residue-blocks** [claim](#stmt-lem-global-residue-blocks) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-global-residue-blocks): product decomposition over blocks -> product of sublevels
- **s-product-minus-top** [claim](#stmt-lem-global-product-minus-top) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-global-product-minus-top): nerve cover and Quillen Theorem A -> contractible deleted product
- **s-lower-link** [claim](#stmt-prop-global-lower-link) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-global-lower-link): combine retraction, product and deleted product -> contractible lower part
- **s-deligne-contractible** [claim](#stmt-thm-deligne-contractible) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-deligne-contractible): vertex filtration plus rank-one connectedness -> contractible coset poset
- **s-main** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-deligne-contractible-2): comparison lemma applied to contractibility -> aspherical Salvetti complex
- **s-artin-structural** [claim](#stmt-cor-artin-structural) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-artin-structural): finite classifying space, product of components, cited monoid equivalence -> torsion, center, monoid results
- **s-boyd-stability** [claim](#stmt-cor-boyd-stability) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-boyd-stability): verify hypothesis of the cited stability theorem -> stability range

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-unique-harmonic-extension**: For a spherical subset T, prescribing the coordinates outside T determines exactly one vector that is harmonic on T, meaning its Cartan rows indexed by T vanish.
- **inv-nonnegative-extension**: Nonnegative prescribed exterior coordinates give a nonnegative harmonic extension on a spherical subset.
- **inv-spherical-iff-positive-definite**: A subset is spherical exactly when its Cartan submatrix is positive definite, so a failure of positive definiteness signals a non-spherical subset on which harmonic extension is not available.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-nonspherical-harmonic**: Run the harmonic extension on a subset whose Cartan submatrix is singular or indefinite, for instance a triangle of unit edges or an infinite-label pair. -> The linear system has no unique solution, so any returned vector is not a well-defined harmonic extension.
- **anti-asphericity-from-code**: Treat a successful finite harmonic-extension computation as evidence for contractibility of the universal cover of the Salvetti complex. -> The computation touches only one linear-algebra lemma; the contractibility theorem needs the whole categorical, layer and filtration argument over infinite posets.
- **anti-irrational-weights**: Feed edge weights from finite labels at least four into the exact rational routine by rounding them to integers. -> The diagram weights for such labels are irrational cosines, so rounded inputs give a different matrix and a wrong extension.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `algorithmic`. The harmonic extension on a spherical subset is an explicit finite linear-algebra procedure: test positive definiteness of the Cartan submatrix, then solve the linear system with the exterior coordinates as data. The harness checks it exactly on small diagrams with integer weights; it does not touch the infinite topological argument.

```python
# INPUTS: stdin tokens: n; n rows of symmetric integer weights b (0,1,2) with zero diagonal; m then m indices of T; n exterior values (entries at T ignored)
# PRECONDITIONS: n>=1, b symmetric, T distinct indices, exterior values nonnegative integers
# OUTPUTS: n space separated exact rationals (harmonic extension) or the word NOTSPH
# TIME: O(m^4) via leading minors plus O(m^3) for the solve
# SPACE: O(n^2)
# ARITHMETIC: exact fractions.Fraction, no floating point
# FAILURE: prints NOTSPH when the Cartan submatrix on T is not positive definite
import sys
from fractions import Fraction

def det(M):
    k = len(M)
    M = [r[:] for r in M]
    d = Fraction(1)
    for c in range(k):
        piv = None
        for r in range(c, k):
            if M[r][c] != 0:
                piv = r
                break
        if piv is None:
            return Fraction(0)
        if piv != c:
            M[c], M[piv] = M[piv], M[c]
            d = -d
        d *= M[c][c]
        for r in range(c + 1, k):
            f = M[r][c] / M[c][c]
            for cc in range(c, k):
                M[r][cc] -= f * M[c][cc]
    return d

def solve(A, y):
    k = len(A)
    M = [A[i][:] + [y[i]] for i in range(k)]
    for c in range(k):
        piv = next(r for r in range(c, k) if M[r][c] != 0)
        M[c], M[piv] = M[piv], M[c]
        pv = M[c][c]
        M[c] = [v / pv for v in M[c]]
        for r in range(k):
            if r != c:
                f = M[r][c]
                M[r] = [M[r][t] - f * M[c][t] for t in range(k + 1)]
    return [M[i][k] for i in range(k)]

def main():
    tok = sys.stdin.read().split()
    pos = 0
    n = int(tok[pos]); pos += 1
    b = []
    for i in range(n):
        b.append([int(x) for x in tok[pos:pos + n]])
        pos += n
    m = int(tok[pos]); pos += 1
    T = [int(x) for x in tok[pos:pos + m]]
    pos += m
    p = [Fraction(int(x)) for x in tok[pos:pos + n]]
    inT = set(T)
    A = [[Fraction(2) if i == j else Fraction(-b[i][j]) for j in T] for i in T]
    for k in range(1, m + 1):
        if det([row[:k] for row in A[:k]]) <= 0:
            print('NOTSPH'); return
    rhs = [sum(b[i][j] * p[j] for j in range(n) if j not in inT) for i in T]
    x = solve(A, rhs)
    for idx, i in enumerate(T):
        p[i] = x[idx]
    print(' '.join(str(v) for v in p))

main()
```

    "oracle_code": "import sys\nfrom fractions import Fraction\n\ndef main():\n    inp, outp, expected = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2], sys.argv[3]\n    tok = open(inp).read().split()\n    n = int(tok[0])\n    w = [[int(tok[1 + i * n + j]) for j in range(n)] for i in range(n)]\n    base = 1 + n * n\n    m = int(tok[base])\n    T = [int(t) for t in tok[base + 1: base + 1 + m]]\n    ext = [Fraction(int(t)) for t in tok[base + 1 + m: base + 1 + m + n]]\n    got = open(outp).read().split()\n    M = [[Fraction(2 if a == c else -w[a][c]) for c in T] for a in T]\n    spherical = True\n    for c in range(m):\n        piv = M[c][c]\n        if piv <= 0:\n            spherical = False\n            break\n        for r in range(c + 1, m):\n            f = M[r][c] / piv\n            for cc in range(c, m):\n                M[r][cc] -= f * M[c][cc]\n    if not spherical:\n        ok = got == ['NOTSPH'] and expected.split() == ['NOTSPH']\n        sys.exit(0 if ok else 1)\n    if got == ['NOTSPH'] or len(got) != n:\n        sys.exit(1)\n    try:\n        vals = [Fraction(g) for g in got]\n        exp = [Fraction(e) for e in expected.split()]\n    except Exception:\n        sys.exit(1)\n    for a in range(n):\n        if a not in T and vals[a] != ext[a]:\n            sys.exit(1)\n    for a in T:\n        row = 2 * vals[a] - sum(w[a][c] * vals[c] for c in range(n) if c != a)\n        if row != 0 or vals[a] < 0:\n            sys.exit(1)\n    if exp != vals:\n        sys.exit(1)\n    sys.exit(0)\n\nmain()\n",
    "output_contract": "Either the single word NOTSPH when the Cartan submatrix 2I minus b restricted to T is not positive definite, or n space separated exact rationals (integer or a/b) forming the vector equal to the input outside T and with vanishing Cartan rows on T.",
    "reason": "The harmonic extension on a spherical subset is an explicit finite linear-algebra procedure: test positive definiteness of the Cartan submatrix, then solve the linear system with the exterior coordinates as data. The harness checks it exactly on small diagrams with integer weights; it does not touch the infinite topological argument.",
    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: stdin tokens: n; n rows of symmetric integer weights b (0,1,2) with zero diagonal; m then m indices of T; n exterior values (entries at T ignored)\n# PRECONDITIONS: n>=1, b symmetric, T distinct indices, exterior values nonnegative integers\n# OUTPUTS: n space separated exact rationals (harmonic extension) or the word NOTSPH\n# TIME: O(m^4) via leading minors plus O(m^3) for the solve\n# SPACE: O(n^2)\n# ARITHMETIC: exact fractions.Fraction, no floating point\n# FAILURE: prints NOTSPH when the Cartan submatrix on T is not positive definite\nimport sys\nfrom fractions import Fraction\n\ndef det(M):\n    k = len(M)\n    M = [r[:] for r in M]\n    d = Fraction(1)\n    for c in range(k):\n        piv = None\n        for r in range(c, k):\n            if M[r][c] != 0:\n                piv = r\n                break\n        if piv is None:\n            return Fraction(0)\n        if piv != c:\n            M[c], M[piv] = M[piv], M[c]\n            d = -d\n        d *= M[c][c]\n        for r in range(c + 1, k):\n            f = M[r][c] / M[c][c]\n            for cc in range(c, k):\n                M[r][cc] -= f * M[c][cc]\n    return d\n\ndef solve(A, y):\n    k = len(A)\n    M = [A[i][:] + [y[i]] for i in range(k)]\n    for c in range(k):\n        piv = next(r for r in range(c, k) if M[r][c] != 0)\n        M[c], M[piv] = M[piv], M[c]\n        pv = M[c][c]\n        M[c] = [v / pv for v in M[c]]\n        for r in range(k):\n            if r != c:\n                f = M[r][c]\n                M[r] = [M[r][t] - f * M[c][t] for t in range(k + 1)]\n    return [M[i][k] for i in range(k)]\n\ndef main():\n    tok = sys.stdin.read().split()\n    pos = 0\n    n = int(tok[pos]); pos += 1\n    b = []\n    for i in range(n):\n        b.append([int(x) for x in tok[pos:pos + n]])\n        pos += n\n    m = int(tok[pos]); pos += 1\n    T = [int(x) for x in tok[pos:pos + m]]\n    pos += m\n    p = [Fraction(int(x)) for x in tok[pos:pos + n]]\n    inT = set(T)\n    A = [[Fraction(2) if i == j else Fraction(-b[i][j]) for j in T] for i in T]\n    for k in range(1, m + 1):\n        if det([row[:k] for row in A[:k]]) <= 0:\n            print('NOTSPH'); return\n    rhs = [sum(b[i][j] * p[j] for j in range(n) if j not in inT) for i in T]\n    x = solve(A, rhs)\n    for idx, i in enumerate(T):\n        p[i] = x[idx]\n    print(' '.join(str(v) for v in p))\n\nmain()\n",
